# Statistics and ethics round 3 revision 1 — full clinically anchored review

Date: 2026-10-16. Revision of the Phase 2A focused bank following the manager’s anchor-repetition review. **All rewritten content remains `needs_clinician_review`; clinician approval has not been given.**

**KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:** A is the key in every choice list below. Runtime randomizes all answer choices. This document is an owner review artifact, not gameplay ordering.

All **118 rewritten variants across 30 concepts** are listed, grouped by topic: 72 main statistics/QI, 40 main ethics, four RCA/FMEA, and two developmental-assent variants. There are 114 existing cases; the four interpreter → consent sequences are retained. P2-E clinical pairings are a separate later milestone and have not been created.

**Humor tags:** 59 Situational / 59 Straight. The split is 46/72 statistics, 12/40 ethics, 0/4 RCA/FMEA, and 1/2 assent. Comedy comes from recognizable behavior around the situation, with a separate premise for each tagged variant. Each comic passage can be removed without changing the answer logic; the choices contain no jokes. Capacity, surrogate decisions, DNR, disclosure, clinician fitness, and coercion remain straight.

Names below illustrate materialized runtime text. Patient ages and sexes match each case's first authored profile; the other profiles remain available under the same rules. Spanish interpreter profiles stay Female with ages 43 and 68. Lily/Ruby, their parents and their art identities are unchanged. Each employee-hosted discussion names the staff lead and a real clinical research question. These 60 discussions contain no second patient introduction or generated patient demographics. The 58 patient decision nodes retain their existing patient profiles.

For a reached consent node, the story shown is its new current update; the same patient, age, sex, and complaint are inherited from the interpreter step. The earlier Spanish story remains in the encounter. Practice exhibits retain their numerical inputs and method assumptions. Study endpoints, units and follow-up now fit their clinical questions; no exhibit predicts a named patient’s risk.

Editorial scale: materialized stories 55–60 words; visible lessons 47–55; distinct fallback explanations 21–31. Counts are editorial checks, not clinical approval.

## Select an independent-samples t test

Concept: `concept.statistics-ethics.independent-t`. 4 variants; objective and stable identity retained.

### `question.statistics-ethics.independent-t.v1`

**Humor:** Situational.

**Diagnosis anchor:** Venous leg ulcer

**Complaint:** venous leg ulcer study

**Investigational premise:** Compression device (device).

**Person and setting:** Dana Rivera; staff discussion lead. L2+ with a hired eligible clinical host.

**Story:** Dana Rivera, the journal-club lead, reviews a trial of a new compression device for venous leg ulcers in adults whose arterial assessment permits compression. Two groups contribute healing times. The presenter cites his own testimonial as independent confirmation; he submitted it under his middle name so the reviews would look less repetitive. The protocol's assumptions are available.

**Exhibit — Healing time — illustrative clinical study:** Invented protocol observations for an unnamed investigational compression device in venous leg ulcers. Outcome: healing time, in days. Two separately sampled treatment groups contribute one measurement per person. Groups do not overlap and nobody is matched. Measurements are approximately normal with comparable variability and no influential outliers. All times are observed to completion with no censoring. These practice assumptions supply no real treatment efficacy.

**Question:** Which analysis best fits the study's comparison of mean healing time, using the measurements and assumptions in the exhibit?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. Two-sample t test **(key)**

Rationale: The study compares means from two groups of different people, and its stated continuous-data assumptions are reasonable. A two-sample t procedure matches that structure.

B. Paired t test

Rationale: A paired t procedure needs repeated or deliberately matched measurements. This comparison does not supply those links between observations.

C. Wilcoxon signed-rank test

Rationale: Signed-rank needs repeated or matched observations from which to form differences. The groups in this comparison do not supply those links.

D. Wilcoxon rank-sum test

Rationale: Rank-sum answers a ranked-observation comparison. This study explicitly requests means and supplies reasonable assumptions for the mean-based procedure.

**Visible teachingPoint:** Start with the outcome and who contributed it. This study compares mean healing time between two groups of different people, with reasonable normal-model assumptions and complete follow-up. A two-sample t test fits that question. Remember: sharing an outcome measure does not make observations linked; repeated people or deliberate matching would change the structure.

**Short explanation fallback:** Use a two-sample t test for mean completed healing time: the groups contain different people, and the stated continuous-data assumptions are reasonable.

**Existing claim bindings:** `claim.gs028se.statistics.independent-t`, `claim.venous-leg-ulcer.arterial-assessment-before-compression`, `claim.venous-leg-ulcer.compression-after-adequate-arterial-assessment`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.paired-t`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.rank-sum`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.signed-rank`.

**Existing sources:** <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/eda/section3/eda353.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.independent-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/prc/section3/prc311.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.paired-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/ranksum.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.rank-sum.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/signrank.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.signed-rank.2026</a>; <a href="https://esvs.org/guideline/chronic-venous-disease-of-lower-limbs/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.vlu.esvs-cvd.2022</a>.

### `question.statistics-ethics.independent-t.v2`

**Humor:** Straight.

**Diagnosis anchor:** Lactational breast abscess

**Complaint:** lactational breast abscess study

**Investigational premise:** Topical wound or skin agent (drug).

**Person and setting:** Alex Morgan; staff discussion lead. L2+ with a hired eligible clinical host.

**Story:** Alex Morgan, the breast-service research lead, reviews a trial of a new topical agent after drainage of lactational breast abscesses. Different participants received each preparation. Ultrasound measured the remaining cavity area at the protocol visit. A colleague proposes linking the groups because the same sonographer collected all measurements. The report includes the distribution checks for analysis.

**Exhibit — Remaining abscess-cavity area — illustrative clinical study:** Invented protocol observations for an unnamed investigational topical agent in lactational breast abscesses. Outcome: remaining abscess-cavity area, in square centimeters. Two separately sampled treatment groups contribute one measurement per person. Groups do not overlap and nobody is matched. Measurements are approximately normal with comparable variability and no influential outliers. These practice assumptions supply no real treatment efficacy.

**Question:** Which analysis best fits the study's comparison of mean remaining abscess-cavity area, using the measurements and assumptions in the exhibit?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. Two-sample t test **(key)**

Rationale: The study compares means from two groups of different people, and its stated continuous-data assumptions are reasonable. A two-sample t procedure matches that structure.

B. Paired t test

Rationale: A paired t procedure needs repeated or deliberately matched measurements. This comparison does not supply those links between observations.

C. Wilcoxon rank-sum test

Rationale: Rank-sum answers a ranked-observation comparison. This study explicitly requests means and supplies reasonable assumptions for the mean-based procedure.

D. Chi-square test of independence

Rationale: Chi-square testing analyzes counts in categories. The requested comparison concerns continuous measurements or ordered ratings, not a categorical contingency table.

**Visible teachingPoint:** The scanner does not define the relationship between observations. These abscess-cavity areas come from different people in two nonoverlapping groups, with suitable continuous-data assumptions. A two-sample t test compares their means. Remember to ask who was measured, not whether the groups used the same instrument or had the same number of columns.

**Short explanation fallback:** A two-sample t test fits mean remaining abscess-cavity area in two nonoverlapping groups. Using the same sonographer does not create repeated or matched observations.

**Existing claim bindings:** `claim.gs028se.statistics.independent-t`, `claim.lactational-breast-abscess.targeted-ultrasound`, `claim.lactational-breast-abscess.selected-drainage`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.chi-square`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.paired-t`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.rank-sum`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.independent-t.mcnemar-boundary`.

**Existing sources:** <a href="https://abm.memberclicks.net/assets/DOCUMENTS/PROTOCOLS/36-mitchell-et-al-2022-academy-of-breastfeeding-medicine-clinical-protocol-36-the-mastitis-spectrum-revised-2022.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.expansion.abm-mastitis-2022</a>; <a href="https://acsearch.acr.org/docs/3196809/Narrative" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.expansion.acr-lactation-imaging-2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/chistest.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.chi-square.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/eda/section3/eda353.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.independent-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/mcnemar.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.mcnemar.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/prc/section3/prc311.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.paired-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/ranksum.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.rank-sum.2026</a>.

### `question.statistics-ethics.independent-t.v3`

**Humor:** Straight.

**Diagnosis anchor:** Superficial partial-thickness burn

**Complaint:** superficial partial-thickness burn study

**Investigational premise:** Burn cooling dressing (device).

**Person and setting:** Riley Chen; staff discussion lead. L2+ with a hired eligible clinical host.

**Story:** Riley Chen, the burn-service discussion lead, reviews two new cooling dressings for superficial partial-thickness burns. The trial records remaining wound depth at a protocol visit, with each adult contributing one measurement. A colleague has prepared a comparison of dressing labels. The investigators' stated question concerns average remaining depth, and the measurement assumptions appear in the exhibit.

**Exhibit — Remaining burn depth — illustrative clinical study:** Invented protocol observations for an unnamed investigational cooling dressing in superficial partial-thickness burns. Outcome: remaining burn depth, in millimeters. Two separately sampled treatment groups contribute one measurement per person. Groups do not overlap and nobody is matched. Measurements are approximately normal with comparable variability and no influential outliers. These practice assumptions supply no real treatment efficacy.

**Question:** Which analysis best fits the study's comparison of mean remaining burn depth, using the measurements and assumptions in the exhibit?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. Two-sample t test **(key)**

Rationale: The study compares means from two groups of different people, and its stated continuous-data assumptions are reasonable. A two-sample t procedure matches that structure.

B. Wilcoxon signed-rank test

Rationale: Signed-rank needs repeated or matched observations from which to form differences. The groups in this comparison do not supply those links.

C. Wilcoxon rank-sum test

Rationale: Rank-sum answers a ranked-observation comparison. This study explicitly requests means and supplies reasonable assumptions for the mean-based procedure.

D. Chi-square test of independence

Rationale: Chi-square testing analyzes counts in categories. The requested comparison concerns continuous measurements or ordered ratings, not a categorical contingency table.

**Visible teachingPoint:** Device names identify the groups; measured burn depth is the continuous outcome. With two groups of different people and reasonable normal-model assumptions, a two-sample t test fits a comparison of means. Remember to distinguish a group label from the outcome being compared before selecting a categorical or continuous-data procedure.

**Short explanation fallback:** Compare mean burn depth with a two-sample t test under the supplied assumptions. Device labels define groups; they are not the measured outcome.

**Existing claim bindings:** `claim.gs028se.statistics.independent-t`, `claim.burn.superficial-partial-depth`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.chi-square`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.rank-sum`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.signed-rank`.

### `question.statistics-ethics.independent-t.v4`

**Humor:** Situational.

**Diagnosis anchor:** Chronic anal fissure

**Complaint:** chronic anal fissure study

**Investigational premise:** Topical wound or skin agent (drug).

**Person and setting:** Jordan Brooks; staff discussion lead. L2+ with a hired eligible clinical host.

**Story:** Jordan Brooks, the colorectal research lead, reviews a study of a new topical agent for chronic anal fissure. Two hospitals contribute completed healing times from different patients. The company representative calls his presentation a brief overview, then asks whether attending it can count toward everyone's continuing education credits. The report supplies its sampling and distribution details.

**Exhibit — Fissure-healing time — illustrative clinical study:** Invented protocol observations for an unnamed investigational topical agent in chronic anal fissure. Outcome: fissure-healing time, in days. Two hospitals using different study preparations contribute different people, without matching, with one completed healing time per person. Measurements are approximately normal with comparable variability and no influential outliers. All times are observed to completion with no censoring. These practice assumptions supply no real treatment efficacy.

**Question:** Which analysis best fits the study's comparison of mean fissure-healing time, using the measurements and assumptions in the exhibit?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. Two-sample t test **(key)**

Rationale: The study compares means from two groups of different people, and its stated continuous-data assumptions are reasonable. A two-sample t procedure matches that structure.

B. Paired t test

Rationale: A paired t procedure needs repeated or deliberately matched measurements. This comparison does not supply those links between observations.

C. Wilcoxon signed-rank test

Rationale: Signed-rank needs repeated or matched observations from which to form differences. The groups in this comparison do not supply those links.

D. Chi-square test of independence

Rationale: Chi-square testing analyzes counts in categories. The requested comparison concerns continuous measurements or ordered ratings, not a categorical contingency table.

**Visible teachingPoint:** A two-sample t test compares means from two groups of different people when the stated assumptions are reasonable. Each participant contributes one completed fissure-healing time here, with no matching. Remember that two columns alone tell you little: the people, measurement scale and distribution checks determine whether a two-group mean comparison fits.

**Short explanation fallback:** A two-sample t test fits these two groups' mean completed fissure-healing times. There is no matching, and the normal-model assumptions are stated to be reasonable.

## Select a paired t test

Concept: `concept.statistics-ethics.paired-t`. 4 variants; objective and stable identity retained.

### `question.statistics-ethics.paired-t.v1`

**Humor:** Situational.

**Diagnosis anchor:** Hidradenitis suppurativa

**Complaint:** hidradenitis suppurativa study

**Investigational premise:** Topical wound or skin agent (drug).

**Person and setting:** Sam Patel; staff discussion lead. L2+ with a hired eligible clinical host.

**Story:** Sam Patel, the skin-service research lead, reviews a new topical agent for hidradenitis suppurativa. Investigators measured involved skin area before and after treatment in the same adults. The coordinator describes appointments as flexible: participants may choose any date, provided it is the one already booked for them. The study reports individual changes and their distribution checks.

**Exhibit — Involved-skin-area change — illustrative clinical study:** Invented protocol observations for an unnamed investigational topical agent in hidradenitis suppurativa. Outcome: involved-skin-area change, in square centimeters. The same 18 people are measured before and after treatment. Within-person differences are approximately normal without influential outliers. These practice assumptions supply no real treatment efficacy.

**Question:** Which analysis best fits the study's comparison of mean involved-skin-area change, using the measurements and assumptions in the exhibit?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. Paired t test **(key)**

Rationale: Each change comes from repeated measurements or a designated pair, and those differences are approximately normal. A paired t procedure compares their mean.

B. Independent-samples t test

Rationale: A two-sample t procedure treats the groups as unrelated. It would discard the within-person or designated-pair relationship that supplies this study's differences.

C. Wilcoxon signed-rank test

Rationale: Signed-rank uses the signs and ranks of differences. Here the stated question is about mean change and the approximately normal differences support a paired mean model.

D. Wilcoxon rank-sum test

Rationale: Rank-sum compares ranked observations from unrelated groups. It would ignore the repeated-person or matching structure used to form the study's differences.

**Visible teachingPoint:** Repeated measurements from one person belong together. Calculate each person's involved-skin-area change, then analyze those differences; here they are approximately normal, supporting a paired t test. Remember: the unit of analysis is the within-person change. Treating the visits as unrelated groups discards the relationship built into the study design.

**Short explanation fallback:** Use a paired t test on each person's involved-skin-area change. The same people supply both measurements, and the changes are approximately normal.

**Existing claim bindings:** `claim.gs028se.statistics.paired-t`, `claim.hs.pattern-recognition`, `claim.hs.multimodal-planning`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.independent-t`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.rank-sum`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.signed-rank`.

**Existing sources:** <a href="https://doi.org/10.1111/ajd.14388" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.hs.australasian-2025</a>; <a href="https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9131894/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.hs.north-american-2019</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/eda/section3/eda353.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.independent-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/prc/section3/prc311.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.paired-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/ranksum.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.rank-sum.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/signrank.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.signed-rank.2026</a>.

### `question.statistics-ethics.paired-t.v2`

**Humor:** Situational.

**Diagnosis anchor:** Uncomplicated cutaneous abscess

**Complaint:** cutaneous abscess study

**Investigational premise:** Abscess irrigation instrument (device).

**Person and setting:** Casey Bennett; staff discussion lead. L2+ with a hired eligible clinical host.

**Story:** Casey Bennett, the surgical research lead, reviews a new irrigation instrument used during drainage of cutaneous abscesses. The same participants had cavity depth measured before and after use. The presenter promises an anonymous discussion, then asks everyone to introduce themselves with names, departments and line managers. The report supplies the distribution of each participant's measured change.

**Exhibit — Abscess-cavity-depth change — illustrative clinical study:** Invented protocol observations for an unnamed investigational irrigation instrument in cutaneous abscesses. Outcome: abscess-cavity-depth change, in millimeters. The same 24 people are measured before and after treatment. Within-person differences are approximately normal without influential outliers. These practice assumptions supply no real treatment efficacy.

**Question:** Which analysis best fits the study's comparison of mean abscess-cavity-depth change, using the measurements and assumptions in the exhibit?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. Paired t test **(key)**

Rationale: Each change comes from repeated measurements or a designated pair, and those differences are approximately normal. A paired t procedure compares their mean.

B. Wilcoxon rank-sum test

Rationale: Rank-sum compares ranked observations from unrelated groups. It would ignore the repeated-person or matching structure used to form the study's differences.

C. One-way ANOVA

Rationale: The ordinary unrelated-group ANOVA listed here does not use the designated within-person or matched differences. Extra columns do not remove that linkage.

D. Independent-samples t test

Rationale: A two-sample t procedure treats the groups as unrelated. It would discard the within-person or designated-pair relationship that supplies this study's differences.

**Visible teachingPoint:** The key distribution check concerns within-person changes, not whether each visit's column looks normal separately. These abscess-cavity-depth changes are approximately normal, supporting a paired t test of mean change. Remember to keep each person's two measurements together; a new visit does not create a new group of unrelated people.

**Short explanation fallback:** The approximately normal within-person abscess-cavity-depth changes support a paired t test. Separate visits do not make the repeated observations into unrelated samples.

**Existing claim bindings:** `claim.gs028se.statistics.paired-t`, `claim.bread-butter.cutaneous-abscess.incision-drainage`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.independent-t`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.one-way-anova`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.rank-sum`.

**Existing sources:** <a href="https://www.acep.org/sonoguide/procedures/abscess-evaluation" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.bread-butter.acep-sonoguide-abscess</a>; <a href="https://www.idsociety.org/practice-guideline/skin-and-soft-tissue-infections/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.bread-butter.idsa-ssti-2014</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/prc/section4/prc422.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.anova.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/eda/section3/eda353.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.independent-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/prc/section3/prc311.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.paired-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/ranksum.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.rank-sum.2026</a>.

### `question.statistics-ethics.paired-t.v3`

**Humor:** Straight.

**Diagnosis anchor:** Chronic pilonidal sinus disease

**Complaint:** pilonidal disease study

**Investigational premise:** Pilonidal excision or closure technique (technique).

**Person and setting:** Jamie Reed; staff discussion lead. L2+ with a hired eligible clinical host.

**Story:** Jamie Reed, the colorectal journal-club lead, reviews a new excision technique for chronic pilonidal disease. Investigators matched each recipient with a comparison patient before recording completed healing times. One colleague says, 'They are different people, so the matching cannot matter.' The matching records and the distribution of differences within the designated pairs are available for review.

**Exhibit — Pilonidal-wound healing-time difference — illustrative clinical study:** Invented protocol observations for an unnamed investigational excision technique in chronic pilonidal disease. Outcome: pilonidal-wound healing-time difference, in days. Fifteen treatment recipients were each matched to a comparison recipient before measurement; the 15 differences are computed within those pairs. Within-pair differences are approximately normal without influential outliers. All times are observed to completion with no censoring. These practice assumptions supply no real treatment efficacy.

**Question:** Which analysis best fits the study's comparison of mean pilonidal-wound healing-time difference, using the measurements and assumptions in the exhibit?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. Paired t test **(key)**

Rationale: Each change comes from repeated measurements or a designated pair, and those differences are approximately normal. A paired t procedure compares their mean.

B. Independent-samples t test

Rationale: A two-sample t procedure treats the groups as unrelated. It would discard the within-person or designated-pair relationship that supplies this study's differences.

C. One-way ANOVA

Rationale: The ordinary unrelated-group ANOVA listed here does not use the designated within-person or matched differences. Extra columns do not remove that linkage.

D. Wilcoxon signed-rank test

Rationale: Signed-rank uses the signs and ranks of differences. Here the stated question is about mean change and the approximately normal differences support a paired mean model.

**Visible teachingPoint:** Pairing can come from deliberate matching as well as repeated measurements. Here each technique recipient has a designated comparison partner, and their pilonidal-wound healing-time differences are approximately normal. A paired t test analyzes those differences. Remember: different people can still supply linked observations when the study design establishes the pairs in advance.

**Short explanation fallback:** Use a paired t test for the designated partners' pilonidal-wound healing-time differences. Deliberate matching supplies the linkage, and the differences are approximately normal.

**Existing claim bindings:** `claim.gs028se.statistics.paired-t`, `claim.pilonidal-disease.chronic-sinus-recognition`, `claim.pilonidal-disease.off-midline-closure-planning`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.independent-t`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.one-way-anova`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.signed-rank`.

**Existing sources:** <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/prc/section4/prc422.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.anova.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/eda/section3/eda353.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.independent-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/prc/section3/prc311.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.paired-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/signrank.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.signed-rank.2026</a>; <a href="https://fascrs.org/Web/Web/Patients/Diseases-and-Conditions/A-Z/Pilonidal-Disease.aspx" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.sc.ascrs-pilonidal-patient</a>; <a href="https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8580911/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.sc.siccr-pilonidal-2021</a>.

### `question.statistics-ethics.paired-t.v4`

**Humor:** Situational.

**Diagnosis anchor:** Split-thickness skin-graft donor wound

**Complaint:** skin-graft donor wound study

**Investigational premise:** Skin-graft donor-site dressing (device).

**Person and setting:** Taylor Hayes; staff discussion lead. L2+ with a hired eligible clinical host.

**Story:** Taylor Hayes, the reconstructive research lead, reviews a new dressing for skin-graft donor wounds. The same participants had wound area recorded at baseline and four weeks. The coordinator calls follow-up effortless; participants phone to confirm an email confirming their telephone appointment. A colleague proposes treating visits as separate groups. The report includes the individual wound-area changes.

**Exhibit — Donor-wound-area change — illustrative clinical study:** Invented protocol observations for an unnamed investigational donor-site dressing in skin-graft donor wounds. Outcome: donor-wound-area change, in square centimeters. The same 30 people are measured at baseline and four weeks. Within-person differences are approximately normal without influential outliers. These practice assumptions supply no real treatment efficacy.

**Question:** Which analysis best fits the study's comparison of mean donor-wound-area change, using the measurements and assumptions in the exhibit?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. Paired t test **(key)**

Rationale: Each change comes from repeated measurements or a designated pair, and those differences are approximately normal. A paired t procedure compares their mean.

B. One-way ANOVA

Rationale: The ordinary unrelated-group ANOVA listed here does not use the designated within-person or matched differences. Extra columns do not remove that linkage.

C. Wilcoxon rank-sum test

Rationale: Rank-sum compares ranked observations from unrelated groups. It would ignore the repeated-person or matching structure used to form the study's differences.

D. Wilcoxon signed-rank test

Rationale: Signed-rank uses the signs and ranks of differences. Here the stated question is about mean change and the approximately normal differences support a paired mean model.

**Visible teachingPoint:** Two visits do not mean two unrelated samples when the same people attend both. Use each participant's follow-up-minus-baseline donor-wound area; the approximately normal changes support a paired t test. Remember to analyze the change distribution. A procedure for several unrelated groups would answer a different structural question from this repeated-measurement study.

**Short explanation fallback:** Analyze follow-up-minus-baseline donor-wound area with a paired t test. Both measurements belong to the same people, and their changes meet the supplied normality assumption.

**Existing claim bindings:** `claim.gs028se.statistics.paired-t`, `claim.gs028f.reconstruction.donor-retained-epithelium`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.one-way-anova`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.rank-sum`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.signed-rank`.

**Existing sources:** <a href="https://assets.cureus.com/uploads/review_article/pdf/116718/20221012-8403-1skf9rv.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.gs028f.adnan.skin-grafting.2022</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/prc/section4/prc422.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.anova.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/prc/section3/prc311.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.paired-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/ranksum.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.rank-sum.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/signrank.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.signed-rank.2026</a>.

## Select one-way ANOVA

Concept: `concept.statistics-ethics.one-way-anova`. 4 variants; objective and stable identity retained.

### `question.statistics-ethics.one-way-anova.v1`

**Humor:** Situational.

**Diagnosis anchor:** Clinically uninfected diabetic foot ulcer

**Complaint:** diabetic foot ulcer study

**Investigational premise:** Diabetic-ulcer offloading device (device).

**Person and setting:** Dana Rivera; staff discussion lead. L2+ with a hired eligible clinical host.

**Story:** Dana Rivera, the wound-service research lead, reviews new offloading devices for clinically uninfected diabetic foot ulcers. Three separately assigned groups contribute completed healing times. Each manufacturer wants its product designated the reference standard before anyone sees the results. The investigators request one overall comparison of average healing time, with sampling and distribution information supplied in the report.

**Exhibit — Ulcer-healing time — illustrative clinical study:** Invented protocol observations for an unnamed investigational offloading device in clinically uninfected diabetic foot ulcers. Outcome: ulcer-healing time, in days. 3 separately assigned treatment groups contribute one measurement per person. Groups do not overlap. Model residuals are approximately normal and within-group variances are comparable. All times are observed to completion with no censoring. These practice assumptions supply no real treatment efficacy.

**Question:** Which analysis best fits the study's comparison of mean ulcer-healing time, using the measurements and assumptions in the exhibit?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. One-way ANOVA **(key)**

Rationale: The requested overall mean comparison includes several groups, each containing different people. The residual-normality and comparable-variance assumptions are stated to fit.

B. Paired t test

Rationale: A paired t procedure needs repeated or deliberately matched measurements. This comparison does not supply those links between observations.

C. Wilcoxon rank-sum test

Rationale: Rank-sum addresses two groups rather than the requested overall comparison across several treatment groups. It does not supply that multi-group mean test.

D. Independent-samples t test

Rationale: A two-sample procedure addresses two groups at a time. The requested overall comparison includes more than two treatment groups together.

**Visible teachingPoint:** One-way ANOVA tests an overall question about means across three or more groups when its assumptions fit. These ulcer-healing times come from different people, with suitable residual and spread checks. Remember: one overall test asks whether all group means are equal; a significant result alone would not identify which particular groups differ.

**Short explanation fallback:** Use one-way ANOVA for an overall comparison of mean completed ulcer-healing time across three groups. The stated observation, residual and spread assumptions fit.

**Existing claim bindings:** `claim.gs028se.statistics.one-way-anova`, `claim.dfu.no-antibiotics-uninfected`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.independent-t`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.paired-t`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.rank-sum`.

**Existing sources:** <a href="https://www.idsociety.org/practice-guideline/diabetic-foot-infections/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.dfi.iwgdf-idsa-2023</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/prc/section4/prc422.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.anova.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/eda/section3/eda353.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.independent-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/prc/section3/prc311.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.paired-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/ranksum.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.rank-sum.2026</a>.

### `question.statistics-ethics.one-way-anova.v2`

**Humor:** Situational.

**Diagnosis anchor:** Keloid scar

**Complaint:** keloid study

**Investigational premise:** Injectable scar preparation (drug).

**Person and setting:** Alex Morgan; staff discussion lead. L2+ with a hired eligible clinical host.

**Story:** Alex Morgan, the plastic-surgery research lead, reviews four new injectable preparations for keloid scars. Each participant received one preparation, and scar area was measured at the protocol visit. Procurement wants the winning supplier named before opening bids, so that supplier can help write the purchasing criteria. The report supplies assumptions for comparing the groups' average scar areas.

**Exhibit — Remaining scar area — illustrative clinical study:** Invented protocol observations for an unnamed investigational injectable scar preparation in keloids. Outcome: remaining scar area, in square centimeters. 4 separately assigned treatment groups contribute one measurement per person. Groups do not overlap. Model residuals are approximately normal and within-group variances are comparable. These practice assumptions supply no real treatment efficacy.

**Question:** Which analysis best fits the study's comparison of mean remaining scar area, using the measurements and assumptions in the exhibit?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. One-way ANOVA **(key)**

Rationale: The requested overall mean comparison includes several groups, each containing different people. The residual-normality and comparable-variance assumptions are stated to fit.

B. Independent-samples t test

Rationale: A two-sample procedure addresses two groups at a time. The requested overall comparison includes more than two treatment groups together.

C. Wilcoxon signed-rank test

Rationale: Signed-rank needs repeated or matched observations from which to form differences. The groups in this comparison do not supply those links.

D. Wilcoxon rank-sum test

Rationale: Rank-sum addresses two groups rather than the requested overall comparison across several treatment groups. It does not supply that multi-group mean test.

**Visible teachingPoint:** Four groups require a procedure that addresses their means together. With continuous scar areas, separate participants, reasonable residual normality and comparable variances, one-way ANOVA fits. Remember to count groups after checking the measurement structure. A two-group procedure does not become an overall four-group test simply because the outcome is continuous.

**Short explanation fallback:** One-way ANOVA compares mean remaining scar area across the four groups together. Each participant appears once, and the supplied model assumptions are reasonable.

**Existing claim bindings:** `claim.gs028se.statistics.one-way-anova`, `claim.gs028e.scar_extent`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.independent-t`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.rank-sum`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.signed-rank`.

**Existing sources:** <a href="https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/scars/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.gs028e.nhs_scars</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/prc/section4/prc422.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.anova.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/eda/section3/eda353.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.independent-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/ranksum.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.rank-sum.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/signrank.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.signed-rank.2026</a>.

### `question.statistics-ethics.one-way-anova.v3`

**Humor:** Straight.

**Diagnosis anchor:** Acute superficial perianal abscess

**Complaint:** perianal abscess study

**Investigational premise:** Abscess drainage adjunct (device).

**Person and setting:** Riley Chen; staff discussion lead. L2+ with a hired eligible clinical host.

**Story:** Riley Chen, the colorectal discussion lead, reviews new drainage adjuncts for superficial perianal abscesses. Three groups contribute remaining cavity depths at the protocol visit. Each participant belongs to one group. The investigators request an overall comparison of average depth. A colleague has instead entered the product labels as the outcome; the measurement assumptions accompany the data.

**Exhibit — Remaining abscess-cavity depth — illustrative clinical study:** Invented protocol observations for an unnamed investigational drainage adjunct in perianal abscesses. Outcome: remaining abscess-cavity depth, in millimeters. 3 separately assigned treatment groups contribute one measurement per person. Groups do not overlap. Model residuals are approximately normal and within-group variances are comparable. These practice assumptions supply no real treatment efficacy.

**Question:** Which analysis best fits the study's comparison of mean remaining abscess-cavity depth, using the measurements and assumptions in the exhibit?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. One-way ANOVA **(key)**

Rationale: The requested overall mean comparison includes several groups, each containing different people. The residual-normality and comparable-variance assumptions are stated to fit.

B. Independent-samples t test

Rationale: A two-sample procedure addresses two groups at a time. The requested overall comparison includes more than two treatment groups together.

C. Wilcoxon rank-sum test

Rationale: Rank-sum addresses two groups rather than the requested overall comparison across several treatment groups. It does not supply that multi-group mean test.

D. Chi-square test of independence

Rationale: Chi-square testing analyzes counts in categories. The requested comparison concerns continuous measurements or ordered ratings, not a categorical contingency table.

**Visible teachingPoint:** The device label defines the group, while abscess-cavity depth supplies the measured outcome. One-way ANOVA compares mean depth across these three groups under the stated assumptions. Remember to identify what was measured before choosing a procedure: a table listing group names does not turn a continuous outcome into categorical counts.

**Short explanation fallback:** Use one-way ANOVA to compare mean abscess-cavity depth across three treatment groups. Depth is continuous; treatment labels identify the groups being compared.

**Existing claim bindings:** `claim.gs028se.statistics.one-way-anova`, `claim.bread-butter.perianal-abscess.prompt-drainage`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.chi-square`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.independent-t`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.rank-sum`.

**Existing sources:** <a href="https://www.ascrsu.com/ascrs/view/ASCRS-Toolkit/2851069/all/Management_of_Anorectal_Abscess_Fistula_in_Ano_and_Rectovaginal_Fistula__2022_" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.bread-butter.ascrs-anorectal-abscess-2022</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/prc/section4/prc422.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.anova.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/chistest.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.chi-square.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/eda/section3/eda353.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.independent-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/ranksum.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.rank-sum.2026</a>.

### `question.statistics-ethics.one-way-anova.v4`

**Humor:** Situational.

**Diagnosis anchor:** Adult acute respiratory distress syndrome

**Complaint:** ARDS study

**Investigational premise:** Ventilation technique for ARDS (technique).

**Person and setting:** Jordan Brooks; staff discussion lead. L2+ with a hired eligible clinical host.

**Story:** Jordan Brooks, the critical-care journal-club lead, reviews five investigational ventilation techniques for adults with ARDS. Each group contributes measured changes in arterial oxygen tension. The designer requests an impartial assessment, then explains how impartial reviewers usually describe his favorite technique. Follow-up measurements are complete, and the report supplies assumptions for an overall comparison of average change.

**Exhibit — Arterial-oxygen-tension change — illustrative clinical study:** Invented protocol observations for an unnamed investigational ventilation technique in adult ARDS. Outcome: arterial-oxygen-tension change, in mmHg. 5 separately assigned treatment groups contribute one measurement per person. Groups do not overlap. Model residuals are approximately normal and within-group variances are comparable. These practice assumptions supply no real treatment efficacy.

**Question:** Which analysis best fits the study's comparison of mean arterial-oxygen-tension change, using the measurements and assumptions in the exhibit?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. One-way ANOVA **(key)**

Rationale: The requested overall mean comparison includes several groups, each containing different people. The residual-normality and comparable-variance assumptions are stated to fit.

B. Paired t test

Rationale: A paired t procedure needs repeated or deliberately matched measurements. This comparison does not supply those links between observations.

C. Wilcoxon signed-rank test

Rationale: Signed-rank needs repeated or matched observations from which to form differences. The groups in this comparison do not supply those links.

D. Wilcoxon rank-sum test

Rationale: Rank-sum addresses two groups rather than the requested overall comparison across several treatment groups. It does not supply that multi-group mean test.

**Visible teachingPoint:** One-way ANOVA can compare mean changes across five groups when its assumptions fit. Each participant contributes one oxygen-tension change score to a single group. Remember: the analysis compares group means of those scores; a change score does not make different treatment groups into matched pairs. An overall result does not locate every difference.

**Short explanation fallback:** One-way ANOVA fits the overall comparison of mean arterial-oxygen-tension change across five groups. A significant overall result alone would not identify which groups differ.

**Existing claim bindings:** `claim.gs028se.statistics.one-way-anova`, `claim.gs028d.ards.permeability-edema`, `claim.gs028d.ards.predicted-body-weight-ventilation`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.paired-t`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.rank-sum`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.signed-rank`.

**Existing sources:** <a href="https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00134-023-07050-7" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.esicm.ards-guideline.2023</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/prc/section4/prc422.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.anova.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/prc/section3/prc311.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.paired-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/ranksum.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.rank-sum.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/signrank.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.signed-rank.2026</a>.

## Select a Wilcoxon rank-sum test

Concept: `concept.statistics-ethics.rank-sum`. 4 variants; objective and stable identity retained.

### `question.statistics-ethics.rank-sum.v1`

**Humor:** Situational.

**Diagnosis anchor:** Achalasia after exclusion of structural obstruction

**Complaint:** achalasia study

**Investigational premise:** Endoscopic technique for achalasia (technique).

**Person and setting:** Sam Patel; staff discussion lead. L2+ with a hired eligible clinical host.

**Story:** Sam Patel, the esophageal research lead, reviews two new endoscopic techniques for achalasia. The study uses ordered, protocol-defined symptom-relief ratings after structural obstruction was excluded. The sponsor calls the report accessible; the scale explanation appears only after the tutorial on accessing the report. Each participant received one technique, and the exhibit describes how the ratings are used.

**Exhibit — Symptom-relief ratings — illustrative clinical study:** Invented protocol observations for an unnamed investigational endoscopic technique in achalasia. Outcome: symptom-relief ratings, in ordered categories. Two separately sampled groups contribute one result per person, without matching. The protocol uses a five-level ordinal rating, not assumed equally spaced and not an established clinical score. These practice assumptions supply no real treatment efficacy.

**Question:** Which analysis best fits the study's comparison of symptom-relief ratings, using the measurements and assumptions in the exhibit?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. Wilcoxon rank-sum test **(key)**

Rationale: These protocol-defined symptom-relief ratings are ordinal, and the two groups contain different people. Rank-sum fits that ranked comparison without assuming equal distances between category labels.

B. Wilcoxon signed-rank test

Rationale: Signed-rank needs repeated or matched observations from which to form differences. The groups in this comparison do not supply those links.

C. Paired t test

Rationale: A paired t procedure needs repeated or deliberately matched measurements. This comparison does not supply those links between observations.

D. Independent-samples t test

Rationale: This mean-based procedure does not fit the supplied ordinal scale or unsuitable normal-model distribution. A numerical code or a continuous time measurement alone does not establish its assumptions.

**Visible teachingPoint:** Ordered symptom-relief categories preserve rank without promising equal spacing between labels. A Wilcoxon rank-sum test fits the comparison of two groups of different people here. Remember: ordered is not automatically continuous. Using a ranking procedure also does not justify describing every result as an unqualified difference in population medians.

**Short explanation fallback:** Use Wilcoxon rank-sum for ordinal symptom-relief ratings from two groups of different people. Ordered labels do not establish equal spacing for a mean comparison.

**Existing claim bindings:** `claim.gs028se.statistics.rank-sum`, `claim.achalasia.high-resolution-manometry`, `claim.achalasia.manometric-recognition`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.independent-t`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.paired-t`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.signed-rank`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.rank-sum.mcnemar-boundary`.

**Existing sources:** <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/eda/section3/eda353.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.independent-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/mcnemar.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.mcnemar.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/prc/section3/prc311.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.paired-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/ranksum.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.rank-sum.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/signrank.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.signed-rank.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.jnmjournal.org/journal/view.html?doi=10.5056/jnm20014" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.sc.seoul-achalasia-2020</a>.

### `question.statistics-ethics.rank-sum.v2`

**Humor:** Situational.

**Diagnosis anchor:** Gastroparesis after exclusion of mechanical obstruction

**Complaint:** gastroparesis study

**Investigational premise:** Gastric motility agent (drug).

**Person and setting:** Casey Bennett; staff discussion lead. L2+ with a hired eligible clinical host.

**Story:** Casey Bennett, the foregut journal-club lead, reviews a new gastric motility agent for gastroparesis after mechanical obstruction was excluded. Two groups contribute gastric-emptying times, including verified unusually long measurements. The project manager promises a short discussion, then distributes an agenda with a separate agenda briefing. The report describes the distributions and the comparison requested by investigators.

**Exhibit — Gastric-emptying-time distributions — illustrative clinical study:** Invented protocol observations for an unnamed investigational gastric motility agent in gastroparesis. Outcome: gastric-emptying-time distributions, in minutes. Two separately sampled groups contribute one result per person, without matching. Measurements are strongly right-skewed with verified influential extremes; a normal-model mean comparison is unsuitable. No common distribution shape is established. All times are observed to completion with no censoring. These practice assumptions supply no real treatment efficacy.

**Question:** Which analysis best fits the study's comparison of gastric-emptying-time distributions, using the measurements and assumptions in the exhibit?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. Wilcoxon rank-sum test **(key)**

Rationale: The groups contain different people with completed gastric-emptying times that are unsuitable for the stated normal model. Rank-sum fits this comparison of ranked observations.

B. Independent-samples t test

Rationale: This mean-based procedure does not fit the supplied ordinal scale or unsuitable normal-model distribution. A numerical code or a continuous time measurement alone does not establish its assumptions.

C. Wilcoxon signed-rank test

Rationale: Signed-rank needs repeated or matched observations from which to form differences. The groups in this comparison do not supply those links.

D. Chi-square test of independence

Rationale: Chi-square testing analyzes counts in categories. The requested comparison concerns continuous measurements or ordered ratings, not a categorical contingency table.

**Visible teachingPoint:** Check the data structure before the distribution. These completed gastric-emptying times come from two groups of different people, but verified extreme skew makes the stated normal-model comparison unsuitable. Wilcoxon rank-sum fits this ranked comparison. Remember that a median-only interpretation still needs additional distributional assumptions; selecting the procedure does not supply them.

**Short explanation fallback:** Wilcoxon rank-sum fits the two groups' ranked gastric-emptying-time comparison. The verified skew and extremes make the stated normal-model mean comparison unsuitable for this exercise.

**Existing claim bindings:** `claim.gs028se.statistics.rank-sum`, `claim.gastroparesis.diagnostic-framework`, `claim.gastroparesis.four-hour-solid-meal-scintigraphy`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.chi-square`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.independent-t`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.signed-rank`.

**Existing sources:** <a href="https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35926490/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.acg.gastroparesis-guideline.2022</a>; <a href="https://gi.org/topics/gastroparesis/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.acg.gastroparesis-patient-information.2026</a>; <a href="https://gastro.org/clinical-guidance/clinical-guidance-on-the-management-of-gastroparesis/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.aga.gastroparesis-guideline.2025</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/chistest.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.chi-square.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/eda/section3/eda353.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.independent-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/ranksum.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.rank-sum.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/signrank.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.signed-rank.2026</a>.

### `question.statistics-ethics.rank-sum.v3`

**Humor:** Straight.

**Diagnosis anchor:** Carpal tunnel syndrome

**Complaint:** carpal tunnel syndrome study

**Investigational premise:** Wrist-bracing device (device).

**Person and setting:** Jamie Reed; staff discussion lead. L2+ with a hired eligible clinical host.

**Story:** Jamie Reed, the hand-service research lead, reviews two new wrist devices for carpal tunnel syndrome. Participants reported hand function on an ordered scale at the protocol visit. A colleague proposes matching people because both groups used the same form. Another wants to treat the labels as evenly spaced measurements. The study's scale definitions accompany the results.

**Exhibit — Hand-function categories — illustrative clinical study:** Invented protocol observations for an unnamed investigational wrist device in carpal tunnel syndrome. Outcome: hand-function categories, in ordered categories. Two separately sampled groups contribute one result per person, without matching. The protocol uses a seven-level ordinal rating, not assumed equally spaced and not an established clinical score. These practice assumptions supply no real treatment efficacy.

**Question:** Which analysis best fits the study's comparison of hand-function categories, using the measurements and assumptions in the exhibit?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. Wilcoxon rank-sum test **(key)**

Rationale: These protocol-defined hand-function ratings are ordinal, and the two groups contain different people. Rank-sum fits that ranked comparison without assuming equal distances between category labels.

B. Wilcoxon signed-rank test

Rationale: Signed-rank needs repeated or matched observations from which to form differences. The groups in this comparison do not supply those links.

C. One-way ANOVA

Rationale: This ordinary mean-based ANOVA does not address the supplied ordinal or unsuitable non-normal comparison. Having groups does not by itself establish its assumptions.

D. Independent-samples t test

Rationale: This mean-based procedure does not fit the supplied ordinal scale or unsuitable normal-model distribution. A numerical code or a continuous time measurement alone does not establish its assumptions.

**Visible teachingPoint:** A shared rating form does not link people in different groups. These protocol-defined categories are ordinal, so Wilcoxon rank-sum fits their two-group comparison. Remember that numbers printed beside ordered labels do not establish equal distances between them. A mean-based model requires more than a convenient numerical code on the form.

**Short explanation fallback:** The groups contain different people using an ordinal scale, supporting Wilcoxon rank-sum. A shared form neither links the observations nor supplies equal measurement intervals.

**Existing claim bindings:** `claim.gs028se.statistics.rank-sum`, `claim.gs028f.nerve.median-carpal-localization`, `claim.gs028f.nerve.median-wrist-symptoms`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.independent-t`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.one-way-anova`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.signed-rank`.

**Existing sources:** <a href="https://medlineplus.gov/genetics/condition/carpal-tunnel-syndrome/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.gs028f.nlm.carpal-tunnel-genetics.2018</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/prc/section4/prc422.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.anova.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/eda/section3/eda353.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.independent-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/ranksum.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.rank-sum.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/signrank.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.signed-rank.2026</a>.

### `question.statistics-ethics.rank-sum.v4`

**Humor:** Straight.

**Diagnosis anchor:** Neuropathic pain after inguinal hernia repair

**Complaint:** postherniorrhaphy pain study

**Investigational premise:** Targeted nerve-block delivery device (device).

**Person and setting:** Taylor Hayes; staff discussion lead. L2+ with a hired eligible clinical host.

**Story:** Taylor Hayes, the pain-service journal-club lead, reviews a new targeted nerve-block delivery device for postherniorrhaphy pain. Two groups contribute the duration of symptom relief, with verified long observations retained. No participants are matched. A colleague proposes describing any detected difference as a difference in medians. The report provides the available distribution checks and the intended comparison.

**Exhibit — Symptom-relief-duration distributions — illustrative clinical study:** Invented protocol observations for an unnamed investigational nerve-block delivery device in postherniorrhaphy pain. Outcome: symptom-relief-duration distributions, in hours. Two separately sampled groups contribute one result per person, without matching. Measurements are strongly right-skewed with verified influential extremes; a normal-model mean comparison is unsuitable. No common distribution shape is established. All times are observed to completion with no censoring. These practice assumptions supply no real treatment efficacy.

**Question:** Which analysis best fits the study's comparison of symptom-relief-duration distributions, using the measurements and assumptions in the exhibit?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. Wilcoxon rank-sum test **(key)**

Rationale: The groups contain different people with completed symptom-relief durations that are unsuitable for the stated normal model. Rank-sum fits this comparison of ranked observations.

B. Paired t test

Rationale: A paired t procedure needs repeated or deliberately matched measurements. This comparison does not supply those links between observations.

C. Wilcoxon signed-rank test

Rationale: Signed-rank needs repeated or matched observations from which to form differences. The groups in this comparison do not supply those links.

D. Independent-samples t test

Rationale: This mean-based procedure does not fit the supplied ordinal scale or unsuitable normal-model distribution. A numerical code or a continuous time measurement alone does not establish its assumptions.

**Visible teachingPoint:** Wilcoxon rank-sum compares ranked observations from two groups of different people. It fits the stated non-normal symptom-relief-duration distributions here. Remember the interpretation boundary: without further shape and location assumptions, its result is not automatically a pure median comparison. Choosing a suitable procedure and describing what it establishes are separate steps.

**Short explanation fallback:** Use Wilcoxon rank-sum for the stated ranked symptom-relief-duration comparison. Its result is not automatically a pure median comparison without additional distributional assumptions.

## Select a Wilcoxon signed-rank test

Concept: `concept.statistics-ethics.signed-rank`. 4 variants; objective and stable identity retained.

### `question.statistics-ethics.signed-rank.v1`

**Humor:** Situational.

**Diagnosis anchor:** Graves hyperthyroidism

**Complaint:** Graves disease study

**Investigational premise:** Thyroid-directed agent (drug).

**Person and setting:** Dana Rivera; staff discussion lead. L2+ with a hired eligible clinical host.

**Story:** Dana Rivera, the endocrine research lead, reviews a new thyroid-directed agent for Graves disease. The same adults had thyroid volume measured before and after treatment. The reviewer requests raw measurements; the coordinator offers the polished version because it makes a better first impression. The report describes the distribution of individual volume changes and the assumptions under review.

**Exhibit — Thyroid-volume change — illustrative clinical study:** Invented protocol observations for an unnamed investigational thyroid-directed agent in Graves disease. Outcome: thyroid-volume change, in cubic centimeters. The same people are measured before and after treatment. Differences are reasonably symmetric but distinctly non-normal, making a paired mean model unsuitable. These practice assumptions supply no real treatment efficacy.

**Question:** Which analysis best fits the study's comparison of thyroid-volume change, using the measurements and assumptions in the exhibit?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. Wilcoxon signed-rank test **(key)**

Rationale: The study supplies linked continuous differences that are reasonably symmetric but unsuitable for a t model. Signed-rank uses their signs and ranks.

B. Wilcoxon rank-sum test

Rationale: Rank-sum compares ranked observations from unrelated groups. It would ignore the repeated-person or matching structure used to form the study's differences.

C. Independent-samples t test

Rationale: A two-sample t procedure treats the groups as unrelated. It would discard the within-person or designated-pair relationship that supplies this study's differences.

D. Paired t test

Rationale: The observations are linked, but the exhibit says their differences are unsuitable for the paired mean model. Linkage alone does not establish its distributional assumptions.

**Visible teachingPoint:** Keep each person's measurements linked before considering the distribution. These thyroid-volume differences are reasonably symmetric but unsuitable for the paired mean model, supporting Wilcoxon signed-rank. Remember: signed-rank uses the signs and ranks of nonzero within-pair differences. It is not the same procedure as comparing ranked observations from unrelated groups.

**Short explanation fallback:** Use Wilcoxon signed-rank for the linked thyroid-volume differences: they are reasonably symmetric but unsuitable for the stated paired mean model in this exercise.

**Existing claim bindings:** `claim.gs028se.statistics.signed-rank`, `claim.graves-rai-appropriate-candidate.graves-context`, `claim.graves-rai-appropriate-candidate.preference-sensitive-options`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.independent-t`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.paired-t`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.rank-sum`.

**Existing sources:** <a href="https://www.thyroid.org/graves-disease/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.ata.graves-disease.undated</a>; <a href="https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/endocrine-diseases/graves-disease" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.niddk.graves-disease.2021</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/eda/section3/eda353.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.independent-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/prc/section3/prc311.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.paired-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/ranksum.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.rank-sum.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/signrank.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.signed-rank.2026</a>.

### `question.statistics-ethics.signed-rank.v2`

**Humor:** Situational.

**Diagnosis anchor:** Primary hyperparathyroidism with renal involvement

**Complaint:** primary hyperparathyroidism study

**Investigational premise:** Calcium-lowering agent (drug).

**Person and setting:** Alex Morgan; staff discussion lead. L2+ with a hired eligible clinical host.

**Story:** Alex Morgan, the endocrine journal-club lead, reviews a new calcium-lowering agent for primary hyperparathyroidism. Serum calcium was measured before and after treatment in the same adults. The presenter insists everyone arrive early for his seminar on respecting colleagues' time. One colleague checked each visit separately; the report also describes the distribution of changes within individual participants.

**Exhibit — Serum-calcium change — illustrative clinical study:** Invented protocol observations for an unnamed investigational calcium-lowering agent in primary hyperparathyroidism. Outcome: serum-calcium change, in mg/dL. The same people are measured before and after treatment. Differences are reasonably symmetric but distinctly non-normal, making a paired mean model unsuitable. These practice assumptions supply no real treatment efficacy.

**Question:** Which analysis best fits the study's comparison of serum-calcium change, using the measurements and assumptions in the exhibit?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. Wilcoxon signed-rank test **(key)**

Rationale: The study supplies linked continuous differences that are reasonably symmetric but unsuitable for a t model. Signed-rank uses their signs and ranks.

B. Wilcoxon rank-sum test

Rationale: Rank-sum compares ranked observations from unrelated groups. It would ignore the repeated-person or matching structure used to form the study's differences.

C. Independent-samples t test

Rationale: A two-sample t procedure treats the groups as unrelated. It would discard the within-person or designated-pair relationship that supplies this study's differences.

D. One-way ANOVA

Rationale: The ordinary unrelated-group ANOVA listed here does not use the designated within-person or matched differences. Extra columns do not remove that linkage.

**Visible teachingPoint:** The relevant distribution is the change within each patient. Here serum-calcium differences are reasonably symmetric but have non-normal tails that make the paired mean model unsuitable. Wilcoxon signed-rank fits the stated conditions. Remember that separate checks of the two visit columns do not replace a check of the differences being analyzed.

**Short explanation fallback:** Wilcoxon signed-rank fits the within-person serum-calcium differences under the supplied symmetry and distribution findings. Checking visit columns separately does not answer that change-distribution question.

**Existing claim bindings:** `claim.gs028se.statistics.signed-rank`, `claim.primary-hyperparathyroidism.biochemical-pattern`, `claim.primary-hyperparathyroidism.renal-bone-surgery-assessment`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.independent-t`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.one-way-anova`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.rank-sum`.

**Existing sources:** <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/prc/section4/prc422.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.anova.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/handbook/eda/section3/eda353.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.independent-t.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/ranksum.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.rank-sum.2026</a>; <a href="https://www.itl.nist.gov/div898/software/dataplot/refman1/auxillar/signrank.htm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.nist.signed-rank.2026</a>; <a href="https://endocrinediseases.org/parathyroid/hyperparathyroidism-treatment/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.sc.aaes-phpt-treatment</a>; <a href="https://www.niddk.nih.gov/health-information/endocrine-diseases/primary-hyperparathyroidism" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.sc.niddk-primary-hyperparathyroidism</a>.

### `question.statistics-ethics.signed-rank.v3`

**Humor:** Straight.

**Diagnosis anchor:** Persistent unexplained adult neck mass

**Complaint:** persistent neck mass study

**Investigational premise:** Neck needle-biopsy technique (technique).

**Person and setting:** Riley Chen; staff discussion lead. L2+ with a hired eligible clinical host.

**Story:** Riley Chen, the head-and-neck research lead, reviews a new needle-biopsy technique for persistent neck masses undergoing malignancy evaluation. Recipients were matched to comparison patients before completed biopsy-site healing times were recorded. The investigators want to compare times within those designated pairs. The report supplies the matching structure and the distribution of differences between paired healing times.

**Exhibit — Biopsy-site healing-time differences — illustrative clinical study:** Invented protocol observations for an unnamed investigational needle-biopsy technique in persistent adult neck masses. Outcome: biopsy-site healing-time differences, in days. Treatment recipients were matched to comparison recipients before measurement; differences are computed within the designated pairs. Differences are reasonably symmetric but distinctly non-normal, making a paired mean model unsuitable. All times are observed to completion with no censoring. These practice assumptions supply no real treatment efficacy.

**Question:** Which analysis best fits the study's comparison of biopsy-site healing-time differences, using the measurements and assumptions in the exhibit?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. Wilcoxon signed-rank test **(key)**

Rationale: The study supplies linked continuous differences that are reasonably symmetric but unsuitable for a t model. Signed-rank uses their signs and ranks.

B. Wilcoxon rank-sum test

Rationale: Rank-sum compares ranked observations from unrelated groups. It would ignore the repeated-person or matching structure used to form the study's differences.

C. Independent-samples t test

Rationale: A two-sample t procedure treats the groups as unrelated. It would discard the within-person or designated-pair relationship that supplies this study's differences.

D. McNemar test

Rationale: McNemar testing concerns paired binary responses. The exhibit instead supplies separate participants or continuous matched differences, so that observation structure does not fit.

**Visible teachingPoint:** Deliberately matched people can form pairs even when nobody is measured twice. These biopsy-site healing-time differences are reasonably symmetric but unsuitable for a t model, supporting Wilcoxon signed-rank. Remember to follow the design's links. McNemar testing concerns paired binary responses, not the continuous completed healing times recorded within these matched pairs.

**Short explanation fallback:** Use Wilcoxon signed-rank for the designated pairs' continuous biopsy-site healing-time differences. Their reasonable symmetry and unsuitable t-model distribution support that choice over unrelated-group or binary-response procedures.

**Existing claim bindings:** `claim.gs028se.statistics.signed-rank`, `claim.gs028f.neck.adult-lateral-malignancy-evaluation`, `claim.gs028f.neck.needle-before-open`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.independent-t`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.rank-sum`, `claim.gs028se.statistics.signed-rank.mcnemar-boundary`.

Investigators measured handgrip force before and after treatment in the same adults. Management calls the project frictionless; opening its presentation requires three passwords and a paper permission slip. The report describes individual force changes and the distribution checks for the proposed analysis.

**Exhibit — Handgrip-force change — illustrative clinical study:** Invented protocol observations for an unnamed investigational elbow-bracing device in cubital tunnel syndrome. Outcome: handgrip-force change, in newtons. The same people are measured before and after treatment. Differences are reasonably symmetric but distinctly non-normal, making a paired mean model unsuitable. These practice assumptions supply no real treatment efficacy.

**Question:** Which analysis best fits the study's comparison of handgrip-force change, using the measurements and assumptions in the exhibit?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. Wilcoxon signed-rank test **(key)**

Rationale: The study supplies linked continuous differences that are reasonably symmetric but unsuitable for a t model. Signed-rank uses their signs and ranks.

B. Paired t test

Rationale: The observations are linked, but the exhibit says their differences are unsuitable for the paired mean model. Linkage alone does not establish its distributional assumptions.

C. Wilcoxon rank-sum test

Rationale: Rank-sum compares ranked observations from unrelated groups. It would ignore the repeated-person or matching structure used to form the study's differences.

D. Chi-square test of independence

Rationale: Chi-square testing analyzes counts in categories. The requested comparison concerns continuous measurements or ordered ratings, not a categorical contingency table.
