**Existing sources:** <a href="https://emergencycarebc.ca/clinical_resource/clinical-summary/fever-in-patients-with-impaired-spleen-function/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.asplenia.emergencycarebc-fever.2026</a>; <a href="https://psnet.ahrq.gov/primer/root-cause-analysis" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.gs028f.ahrq_rca2024</a>; <a href="https://www.patientsafety.va.gov/professionals/onthejob/hfmea.asp" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.gs028f.va_pra2026</a>.

### `question.gs028f.outcomes.rca-versus-fmea-purpose.v4`

**Humor:** Straight.

**Diagnosis anchor:** Unverified preoperative penicillin-allergy label

**Complaint:** Allergy-record review

**Person and setting:** Julian Torres; age 29, Male.

**Story:** Julian Torres, a 29-year-old man, has an unverified penicillin-allergy label and is discussing the original reaction history. The center will introduce a new allergy-record workflow. Its safety team announces healthcare FMEA and will examine possible failures before launch. The patient brings the notice and asks about its purpose; no completed recording event prompted this review.

**Exhibit:** None.

**Question:** Which discussion focus fits healthcare FMEA of the planned allergy-record workflow that Julian Torres asks about?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. Potential failures before a problem occurs **(key)**

Rationale: Healthcare FMEA examines how the planned process could fail before those problems occur. It does not require a completed event as its starting point.

B. Contributors to a completed allergy-recording event

Rationale: That focus starts with a past event and fits the retrospective purpose of RCA. The described meeting is about a planned workflow.

C. Individual blame for a completed allergy-recording error

Rationale: Blame for a past error neither describes the prospective process task nor supplies its focus on potential failures.

D. Prior patients' satisfaction with the allergy-recording encounter

Rationale: Satisfaction may be another question, but it does not describe healthcare FMEA's stated task of anticipating potential process failures.

**Visible teachingPoint:** Healthcare FMEA asks how a planned process might fail before those problems occur. This meeting therefore focuses on potential allergy-recording failures, rather than reconstructing a past event. Remember: look ahead at the process. That is a method classification, not a promise of zero risk or a complete set of scoring and safeguard instructions.

**Short explanation fallback:** Focus on potential failures before the allergy-recording workflow starts. That prospective process purpose defines the healthcare FMEA task described here, without requiring a previous allergy-recording error.

**Existing claim bindings:** `claim.gs028f.safety.rca-retrospective`, `claim.gs028f.safety.fmea-prospective`, `claim.variety3.allergy.label-not-confirmation`, `claim.variety3.allergy.characterize-reaction`.

**Existing sources:** <a href="https://psnet.ahrq.gov/primer/root-cause-analysis" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.gs028f.ahrq_rca2024</a>; <a href="https://www.patientsafety.va.gov/professionals/onthejob/hfmea.asp" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.gs028f.va_pra2026</a>; <a href="https://www.cdc.gov/antibiotic-use/hcp/clinical-signs/index.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.variety3.cdc-penicillin-allergy-2025</a>; <a href="https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10886174/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.variety3.sexton-kuruvilla-allergy-2024</a>.

## Include the child in a nonurgent care discussion

Concept: `concept.pediatric-clinic.developmental-assent-discussion`. 2 variants; objective and stable identity retained.

### `question.pediatric-clinic.child-asks-about-examination.v1`

**Humor:** Situational.

**Diagnosis anchor:** Childhood intermittent groin bulge concerning for inguinal hernia

**Complaint:** Groin-examination questions

**Person and setting:** Lily Walker; age 6, Female. Mother Susan Walker remains in the room; art `level3-roster-v2.021`. Standalone draft status and pediatric capability contract retained.

**Story:** Lily Walker, a 6-year-old girl, visits the clinic with her mother, Susan Walker, who remains in the same room throughout the visit. An intermittent groin bulge prompts nonurgent hernia assessment. Lily asks what the examination involves. 'Mum has already answered, but she hasn't done the examining,' she says. Susan agrees; there is time to explain.

**Exhibit:** None.

**Question:** Which communication approach should be used before Lily Walker's nonurgent groin examination when her mother agrees but Lily has questions?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. Explain to Lily and invite her participation **(key)**

Rationale: Lily has questions before a nonurgent examination. Explain in terms she can understand and invite her participation, while keeping Susan involved rather than treating her agreement as the whole discussion.

B. Explain to Susan and begin the examination

Rationale: Susan's agreement does not answer Lily's questions or provide the child's own developmentally appropriate participation before this nonurgent examination.

C. Accept Lily's silence and begin the examination

Rationale: Silence does not establish meaningful participation. Lily has asked what will happen and needs an understandable explanation and an opportunity for questions.

D. Postpone Lily's involvement until she is older

Rationale: Participation can match Lily's present understanding. A universal older-age requirement would postpone the explanation and invitation this nonurgent visit permits.

**Visible teachingPoint:** Assent means inviting the child's developmentally appropriate participation, not treating a parent's agreement as the end of discussion. Explain the examination in language Lily can understand, invite questions, and seek her participation with Susan involved. Remember to include the child directly. This ethical approach does not establish a universal rule about legal consent authority.

**Short explanation fallback:** Explain the examination directly to Lily, invite questions, and seek developmentally appropriate assent with her mother involved. Her participation and legal consent authority are separate considerations.

**Existing claim bindings:** `claim.pediatric-clinic.developmentally-appropriate-information`, `claim.pediatric-clinic.invite-child-participation-and-assent`, `claim.pediatric-clinic.dynamic-groin-bulge`, `claim.pediatric-clinic.clinical-groin-hernia-referral`, `claim.pediatric-clinic.noncoercive-child-discussion`, `claim.pediatric-clinic.assent-legal-consent-boundary`.

**Existing sources:** <a href="https://rms.cornwall.nhs.uk/rms/primary_care_clinical_referral_criteria/rms/primary_care_clinical_referral_criteria/paediatrics/paediatric_surgery" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.pediatric-clinic.cornwall-surgery-2025</a>; <a href="https://cps.ca/en/documents/position/medical-decision-making-in-paediatrics-infancy-to-adolescence" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.pediatric-clinic.cps-decision-making-2018</a>; <a href="https://www.nhs.uk/conditions/hernia/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.pediatric-clinic.nhs-hernia-2026</a>.

### `question.pediatric-clinic.child-wants-plan-explained.v2`

**Humor:** Straight.

**Diagnosis anchor:** Uncomplicated reactive childhood cervical lymphadenopathy

**Complaint:** Neck-node plan questions

**Person and setting:** Ruby Anderson; age 11, Female. Father Thomas Anderson remains in the room; art `level3-roster-v2.027`. Standalone draft status and pediatric capability contract retained.

**Story:** Ruby Anderson, an 11-year-old girl, visits the clinic with her father, Thomas Anderson, who remains in the same room throughout the visit. Ruby is well with small mobile reactive cervical nodes and no concerning findings. At this nonurgent visit, she asks what observation means and wants the plan explained to her. Thomas asks whether his agreement is enough.

**Exhibit:** None.

**Question:** Which communication approach should be used for Ruby Anderson's nonurgent neck-node observation plan when her father agrees but she wants the plan explained?

**Choices — KEY FIRST FOR REVIEW ONLY:**

A. Explain to Ruby and invite her questions **(key)**

Rationale: Ruby explicitly says she does not understand. Address her directly with an appropriate explanation and invitation for questions and assent, while retaining her father's involvement.

B. Confirm with Thomas and begin the clinic plan

Rationale: Thomas's agreement does not resolve Ruby's stated lack of understanding. Confirming only his view leaves the child's own participation unanswered.

C. Accept Ruby's silence and begin the clinic plan

Rationale: Eventual silence does not establish understanding. The response should give Ruby a meaningful opportunity to understand the plan and express questions or views.

D. Postpone Ruby's involvement until she is older

Rationale: Participation should match Ruby's understanding now. Deferring all involvement to a fixed older age bypasses the understandable discussion she is requesting.

**Visible teachingPoint:** A child's voice can be missed even when a parent is engaged and supportive. Explain the plan directly to Ruby, check understanding, and invite her questions and developmentally appropriate assent with Thomas involved. Remember that parental agreement and child participation address different needs. This ethical participation does not settle jurisdiction-specific legal consent authority.

**Short explanation fallback:** Include Ruby directly, explain the plan understandably, and invite questions and developmentally appropriate assent with her father involved. Her ethical participation and legal consent authority remain separate considerations.

**Existing claim bindings:** `claim.pediatric-clinic.developmentally-appropriate-information`, `claim.pediatric-clinic.invite-child-participation-and-assent`, `claim.pediatric-clinic.reactive-child-neck-nodes`, `claim.pediatric-clinic.well-child-node-observation`, `claim.pediatric-clinic.noncoercive-child-discussion`, `claim.pediatric-clinic.assent-legal-consent-boundary`.

**Existing sources:** <a href="https://rms.cornwall.nhs.uk/primary_care_clinical_referral_criteria/rms/primary_care_clinical_referral_criteria/paediatrics/lymphadenopathy" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.pediatric-clinic.cornwall-nodes-2025</a>; <a href="https://cps.ca/en/documents/position/medical-decision-making-in-paediatrics-infancy-to-adolescence" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">source.pediatric-clinic.cps-decision-making-2018</a>.

## Provenance, review, and withheld boundaries

170 existing atomic claims resolve to 165 existing sources for the focused rewrite. No claim statement, source record, source access date, claim check date, rights assessment or clinician-review status was changed. The prior round 3 ARR/NNT limitation already refers to the stated follow-up horizon; that catalog wording is retained unchanged in this revision. Existing approved clinical claims retain their prior named review; the rewritten questions and cases remain drafts. Added bindings cover the clinical diagnoses, supported operative contexts and care principles as well as method contrasts and ethical distinctions. Catalog records are referenced across batches and are not copied into new evidence catalogs. These are editorial mappings to the existing catalogs, not fresh medical or legal revalidation.

No new source was researched or added. **NEEDS SOURCE — retained surgical sponge remains withheld.** Metastatic colon-cancer regimens, molecular selection and real biologic efficacy remain withheld; the available claims support tissue confirmation and localized resection. Real drug/device effects, established-test numerical performance, personal risk estimates, exact clinical thresholds, universal surrogate hierarchies, interpreter credential rules and jurisdiction-specific adolescent privacy/refusal/consent are also withheld. In particular, `withheld.pediatric-clinic.adolescent-confidentiality` remains withheld. The same-room parent arrangement is the prototype's constraint, not a universal clinical recommendation.

All invented numerical observations and protocol assumptions belong only to unnamed investigational drugs, tests, devices, techniques, care pathways or screening programs. The endpoints fit the clinical question: recurrence, leak, SSI, repeat drainage, diagnostic accuracy, healing or a relevant physiologic measurement. Time-to-completion exercises explicitly stipulate complete uncensored observations; ordinal scales are protocol-defined and not established clinical scores. Constructed method assumptions are not clinical treatment thresholds. No real named drug, trial, guideline or established screening test receives invented performance.

The source limitations remain those in the catalogs, including archived test-accuracy guidance, single-method sources, single-source management/ethics points, older nonbinding QI guidance, and jurisdiction/institution dependence. Automated validation and owner tone review do not approve clinical content.
